Findings:

00416F30 DIP_SCRN::Diplomacy_Generic_System_List_

0x00437290 SHIPMOVE::Update_Ship_ETAs_

fail

- Parameter type does not match: assembly compares `param_1` as a 16-bit value (`CMP AX, [ESP+param_1]`), but the project declares/defines `int32_t player_id`. This is not cosmetic because callers and signedness/width are part of validation.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - void __cdecl Update_Ship_ETAs_(int32_t player_id);
  + void __cdecl Update_Ship_ETAs_(int16_t player_id);
  ```

- The implementation introduces unnecessary 32-bit widening for the destination coordinates and loop comparisons. Assembly keeps the star coordinates and current coordinates as 16-bit locals and uses signed 16-bit loads with `MOVSX` only at compare/call boundaries. This is a likely type restoration issue and should remain in 16-bit space except where C promotion naturally occurs.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - int32_t target_x = (int32_t)star->x;
  - int32_t target_y = (int32_t)star->y;
  + int16_t target_x = star->x;
  + int16_t target_y = star->y;

  - while ((int32_t)current_x != target_x || (int32_t)current_y != target_y) {
  + while (current_x != target_x || current_y != target_y) {
  ```

- Owner argument handling is too loosely typed at the call site. Assembly does `MOVSX AX, byte ptr [ESI+0x63]` then pushes that value; the source forces `(uint8_t)ship->owner`. Since `owner` is signed `char` in the recovered type info, this cast changes semantics for negative owners and does not match the recovered calling sequence.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - GEO::Move_Player_1_Turn_To_Star_((uint8_t)ship->owner, ...)
  + GEO::Move_Player_1_Turn_To_Star_(ship->owner, ...)
  ```
  If the callee prototype is truly `uint8_t`, this needs broader signature/type reconciliation with the original binary before validation can pass.

0x00444FA0 AIDUDES::Do_Blockaded_Colony_

fail

- Uses the wrong qsort comparator. Assembly calls `AIDUDES::Sort_Pops_FI_` at `0x445320`, but the implementation calls `Sort_Pops_FR_I_`. This changes ordering and downstream assignment behavior.
  ```diff
  -        qsort(&colony->pop[(uint8_t)colony_info->first_assignable_pop_idx],
  -              (uint8_t)colony->n_pops - (uint8_t)colony_info->first_assignable_pop_idx,
  -              4,
  -              Sort_Pops_FR_I_);
  +        qsort(&colony->pop[(uint8_t)colony_info->first_assignable_pop_idx],
  +              (uint8_t)colony->n_pops - (uint8_t)colony_info->first_assignable_pop_idx,
  +              4,
  +              Sort_Pops_FI_);
  ```

- In the `food2_per_farmer == 0` path, the job-bit update is not equivalent to the XOR-mask sequence from assembly. The binary toggles bits `0x80/0x100` via `((x ^ mask) & 0x180) ^ x`, preserving whichever target bit is selected from `Event_Check_Colony_Researching_`; current code forcibly clears `0x80` and ORs one mask. That can differ if source state is unexpected or mixed.
  ```diff
  -                colony->pop[(uint8_t)colony_info->cur_pop_idx] = (colony->pop[(uint8_t)colony_info->cur_pop_idx] & 0xFFFFFE7F) | job_mask;
  +                uint32_t& pop = colony->pop[(uint8_t)colony_info->cur_pop_idx];
  +                pop = ((pop ^ job_mask) & 0x180) ^ pop;
  ```

- The parameter type passed to `Event_Check_Colony_Researching_` is broadened unnecessarily: assembly zero-extends the byte colony index to 32-bit before push; current code casts to `uint16_t`. Likely harmless at runtime, but it is not the precise source-level match and suggests type drift.
  ```diff
  -            int16_t is_researching = EVENTS::Event_Check_Colony_Researching_((uint16_t)(uint8_t)colony_info->colony_idx);
  +            int16_t is_researching = EVENTS::Event_Check_Colony_Researching_((int)(uint8_t)colony_info->colony_idx);
  ```

fail

00444E80 AIDUDES::Colony_AI_Given_Tax_

fail

- `_n_our_blockaded` and `_n_our_unblockaded` are typed incorrectly in the project as `uint16_t`, but the assembly uses `MOVSX` and `JLE`, so both counts are signed 16-bit values. This affects exact control-flow semantics for negative values and signed comparisons.
  Suggested diff:
  ```diff
  - inline uint16_t& _n_our_blockaded = *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C0});
  - inline uint16_t& _n_our_unblockaded = *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C4});
  + inline int16_t& _n_our_blockaded = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C0});
  + inline int16_t& _n_our_unblockaded = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C4});
  ```

- Because of the wrong unsigned typing, the two `for` loops in `Colony_AI_Given_Tax_` do not preserve the original signed termination behavior. In the binary, a negative count causes the loop to be skipped due to end-pointer comparison; in the current code, a negative signed memory value reinterpreted as `uint16_t` becomes a huge positive bound.
  Suggested diff:
  ```diff
  - for (int i = 0; i < aidudes::_n_our_blockaded; ++i) {
  + for (int i = 0; i < (int)aidudes::_n_our_blockaded; ++i) {
        aidudes::Do_Blockaded_Colony_(&aidudes::_our_blockaded[i]);
    }

  - for (int i = 0; i < aidudes::_n_our_unblockaded; ++i) {
  + for (int i = 0; i < (int)aidudes::_n_our_unblockaded; ++i) {
        aidudes::Do_Unblockaded_Colony_(&aidudes::_our_unblockaded[i]);
    }
  ```
  But this only becomes correct once the globals are retyped signed.

- The final condition also depends on signedness. The assembly does `CMP word ptr [...],0` followed by `JLE`, i.e. signed `<= 0`. With the current unsigned global type, `if (_n_our_unblockaded > 0)` is not equivalent for negative stored values.
  Suggested diff:
  ```diff
  - if (aidudes::_n_our_unblockaded > 0) {
  + if (aidudes::_n_our_unblockaded > 0) {
        aidudes::Assign_Additional_Unblockaded_Farmers_();
    } else {
        COLCALC::Do_Player_Colony_Post_Production_(aidudes::_i_ai);
    }
  ```
  The source text stays the same, but only after changing `_n_our_unblockaded` to `int16_t` does it match the binary.

fail

0x00444D70 OFFICER::Get_Italian_The_

fail

- The first branch condition is wrong in source: assembly returns HESTR_186_S when `title[0]` is a vowel, but current code returns the literal `", lo "`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  if (MISC::Is_Vowel_(MOX::_leaders[leader_idx].title[0])) {
      strcpy(output, HAROLD::H_Message_(HESTR_186_S));
      return;
  }
  ```

- The special-case leaders `0x1A`, `0x2B`, `0x34` are handled with the wrong string in the vowel path conflation. In assembly these IDs alone use the literal at `0x00556A4C` (`", lo "`), independent of the female/male message path.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  if (leader_idx == 0x1a || leader_idx == 0x2b || leader_idx == 0x34) {
      strcpy(output, s___lo_00556a4c);
      return;
  }
  ```

- The control flow ordering matters and is currently incorrect: assembly checks vowel first, then the three hardcoded IDs, then female, then male. Current implementation changes observable behavior for any of those three leaders whose title begins with a vowel.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  output[0] = '\0';

  if (MISC::Is_Vowel_(MOX::_leaders[leader_idx].title[0])) {
      strcpy(output, HAROLD::H_Message_(HESTR_186_S));
      return;
  }

  if (leader_idx == 0x1a || leader_idx == 0x2b || leader_idx == 0x34) {
      strcpy(output, s___lo_00556a4c);
      return;
  }

  if (Officer_Is_Female_(leader_idx)) {
      strcpy(output, HAROLD::H_Message_(HESTR_182_THE));
      return;
  }

  strcpy(output, HAROLD::H_Message_(HESTR_110_THE));
  ```

fail

The disassembly computes `Officer_Cost_`, keeps only `AX` in `DI`, sign-extends that 16-bit value for the BC comparison and passes a 16-bit cost to `Not_Enough_BCs_Message_`.   mouse::Set_Mouse_List_(&MOX::_mouse_list_arrow, 1);

  ```cpp
  } else {
      Already_Hired_Message_(leader_idx);
  }
  ```

  Suggested diff snippet:

0x00442110 OFFICER::Set_Officer_To_Player_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Replacement-candidate selection uses the wrong ETA comparison direction.
  - Assembly keeps the smallest ETA among status==4 candidates:
    - local_4 starts at 0
    - replace when `local_4 < leader.eta` is false, i.e. when `leader.eta <= current_best`
    - with first candidate always accepted
  - Current code picks the largest ETA:
```cpp
-                            if (leader.status == 4 && (int16_t)leader.eta > max_eta) {
+                            if (leader.status == 4 && (replace_idx == -1 || leader.eta <= max_eta)) {
                                 replace_idx = i;
                                 max_eta = (int16_t)leader.eta;
                             }
```

- Local accumulator type/initialization does not match signed byte ETA semantics from assembly.
  - Assembly stores and compares a signed 16-bit temp against sign-extended `sbyte eta`.
  - Current `max_eta = 0` only works with the already-wrong `>` logic and obscures the original behavior.
```cpp
-        int16_t max_eta = 0;
+        int16_t best_eta = 0;
```

- Status assignment expression is behaviorally correct but obscures exact flag-derived assembly logic (`CMP 1; SBB; AND 4`).
  - Assembly produces 4 when `is_instant == 0`, otherwise 0.
  - Suggest expressing this directly to avoid ambiguity about nonzero values:
```cpp
-        target.status = (is_instant == 0) ? 4 : 0;
+        target.status = is_instant ? 0 : 4;
```

0x00440340 OFFICER::Print_Galmap_Scanned_Ship_

fail

- Owner signedness is wrong. The binary uses `MOVSX` from `ship->owner`, so comparisons and argument passing are signed 8-bit semantics. Current code forces `uint8_t`, changing behavior for values >= 0x80.
- `SYS::N_Ships_By_Type_At_Location_` arguments are swapped versus the call site in assembly. The binary passes pointers in order: total, colony_ships, outposts/bases, transports, hull_counts. Current prototype/use is total, bases, colony_ships, transports, causing `n_outposts` and `n_colony_ships` text to be reversed here.
- The stack-owner/race-name branch condition is off by one. Assembly uses `CMP DI,0x8` / `JLE`, so fallback stack-owner lookup happens only when owner > 8. Current code is correct in branch shape after unsigned cast removal, but with the present unsigned cast it can misroute negative special owners into player-color/race paths incorrectly. This is a correctness risk tied to the same signedness bug above.
- Local buffer/layout behavior does not match the binary exactly for the “named stack” path. Assembly copies the uppercased race name into a second buffer before printing (`local_21c`), while current code prints `race_name` directly. This may be harmless in practice, but because this function uses overlapping fixed stack regions in the original, I cannot confirm exact aliasing/temporary behavior from the restored source as-is.

0x00436f50 SHIPMOVE::Allocate_New_Ship_Slots_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The free-slot scan condition is too tightly coupled to the current C++ struct typing. The assembly checks the raw slot status byte after deriving the slot base from the indexed ship array, and the implementation’s use of `MOX::_ship[i].status == 5` depends on the current `s_ship_data` layout being exact. Because this function also writes `owner = 8` for Antaran-reserved slots, the owner/status field mapping should be confirmed against the binary layout before this can be considered fully validated.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  // ensure exact field intent is documented and consistent with offsets 0x63/0x64
  if (i >= (int)MOX::_NUM_SHIPS || MOX::_ship[i].status == 5) {
  ```

- Signedness/indexing of `colony_production_slots[(uint8_t)col->owner]` is not faithful to the assembly. The binary uses `MOVSX` on `owner` before indexing, not zero-extension. While `owner == -1` is filtered earlier, exact behavior for any negative unexpected owner differs.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  colony_production_slots[(int8_t)col->owner]++;
  ```

- `player_max_slots = colony_production_slots[MOX::_PLAYER_NUM];` uses `_PLAYER_NUM` directly as an array index without preserving the assembly’s signed `MOVSX` semantics. If `_PLAYER_NUM` type or promotion differs in headers, this can change behavior.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  int player_max_slots = colony_production_slots[(int16_t)MOX::_PLAYER_NUM];
  ```

- The round-robin distribution loop was rewritten with `while`/`players_tried`, which is logically close but not instruction-faithful enough to validate with certainty. The assembly increments the fail-counter in `EBX`, resets it only on successful assignment, and checks `NUM_PLAYERS <= EBX` at loop top each pass. I can’t confirm this rewrite preserves all edge cases without seeing surrounding project type definitions for `_NUM_PLAYERS` and exact signed division/modulo expectations.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  int assigned = 0;
  int misses = 0;
  while (assigned < unassigned_count) {
      if (misses >= (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS) break;
      ...
  }
  ```

- Player indexing in the fallback pass uses `MOX::_player[current_player]` directly, but assembly computes player record addresses with a hard-coded packed stride (`3753` bytes). This should normally match, but because the project guarantees packed structs and exact restoration is required, I cannot validate this function fully without confirming the compiled `sizeof(s_player)` is exactly `0xEA9` in this codebase.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  static_assert(sizeof(s_player) == 0xEA9, "s_player size mismatch");
  ```

- Same issue for ship stride: assembly uses `0x81` bytes exactly. Validation requires confirming `sizeof(s_ship_data) == 0x81`; otherwise all indexed accesses are wrong even if the source looks correct.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  static_assert(sizeof(s_ship_data) == 0x81, "s_ship_data size mismatch");
  ```

The main blockers are exact signedness/packing/stride assumptions required by this function’s raw-offset behavior.

0x004363e0 SHIPMOVE::Ships_Try_To_Move_To_

fail

- Address annotation mismatch: the implementation is tagged `@address 00437290`, but the supplied function is at `004363e0`. That requires follow-up because this validation target must match the provided symbol/address pair exactly.
  ```diff
  -    * @address 00437290
  +    * @address 004363e0
  ```

- `move_info` zero-initialization does not follow the exact memory writes from the assembly. The binary clears 14 bytes as 3 dwords + word:
  `blackhole_blocks..moving`, `out_of_range..parsecs`, `uses_wormhole..hyperspace_flux`.
  The source skips `insufficient_fuel`/`pad` explicitly and then overlays an unrelated local for jumpgate state. Semantically it likely ends up zeroed, but the implementation is not precise enough for strict restoration.
  ```diff
  -        move_info->blackhole_blocks = 0;
  -        move_info->immobile = 0;
  -        move_info->turns_left = 0;
  -        move_info->moving = 0;
  -        move_info->out_of_range = 0;
  -        move_info->has_navigator = 0;
  -        move_info->traveling_speed = 0;
  -        move_info->parsecs = 0;
  -        move_info->uses_wormhole = 0;
  -        move_info->has_stargate_route = 0;
  -        move_info->hyperspace_flux = 0;
  +        memset(move_info, 0, sizeof(*move_info));
  ```

- The source models jumpgate routing with a separate local `has_jumpgate_route`, while the binary stores it in `move_info+0x0B`, i.e. the high byte of `uses_wormhole`. This matters because later control flow and finalization read that exact field layout-dependent byte. The current code is functionally similar but not structure-accurate enough for this restoration standard.
  ```diff
  -        int8_t has_jumpgate_route = 0;
  +        /* jumpgate route is stored in ((uint8_t*)&move_info->uses_wormhole)[1] */
  ```

- Navigator flag passed to `GEO::Move_Player_1_Turn_To_Star_` is inverted versus the assembly. The binary passes a non-null pointer only when `navigator_bonus <= 1` (`local_18 < 2`), not when `navigator_bonus != 0`. Current code passes nullptr for no navigator and non-null for any navigator bonus, which changes nebula-stop behavior during ETA simulation.
  ```diff

  +                        (navigator_bonus < 2 ? &dummy_nav_flag : nullptr),
  ```

- Final movement rejection is too broad: the binary only rejects `out_of_range` after first bypassing cases with blackhole block / stargate / wormhole / navigator state in a specific sequence, and the current collapsed condition loses that exact gating. In particular, the assembly allows some black-hole-blocked cases to proceed when jumpgate/stargate/wormhole conditions apply; this should be preserved through the same field-driven logic rather than a simplified boolean.
  ```diff
  -        if ((move_info->blackhole_blocks != 0 && has_jumpgate_route == 0 &&
  -            move_info->has_stargate_route == 0 && navigator_bonus <= 0) ||
  -            (move_info->out_of_range != 0) ||
  -            (ship_speed == 0 && (target_star->x != start_x || target_star->y != start_y))) {
  +        /* mirror assembly's post-processing branches using move_info[0x0B/0x0C] and local navigator bonus */
  ```

- Type/behavior precision issue for `ship_speed`: assembly zero-extends the player `ship_speed` byte when owner < 8. The source reads `MOX::_player[player_idx].ship_speed` directly into signed `int32_t`. If `char` is signed in this build, values >= 128 would sign-extend incorrectly. Even if current game data never hits that range, this is still a signedness mismatch against the binary.
  ```diff
  -            ship_speed = MOX::_player[player_idx].ship_speed;
  +            ship_speed = (uint8_t)MOX::_player[player_idx].ship_speed;
  ```

  ```diff
-     int16_t val = _button_variable[i - 1];
+ for (int i = 1; i < 10; ++i) {
+     int16_t val = _button_variable[i];
  ```

- Zero case in the first loop is wrong. Assembly writes `0` only when `_button_variable[i] == 0`; otherwise it fetches from `MOX::_special_bonus[(i-1)][val]` using the selection value directly as the second index. Current code uses `val - 1`, shifting all mapped values.

  ```diff
+ flags_out[i] = MOX::_special_bonus[i - 1][val];
  ```

  ```diff
      flags_out[15] = -1;
  ```

- Control flow around the 0x0F case is mismatched. Assembly sets a skip flag (`SI=1`), advances DX by an extra 1, and subsequent writes use `ECX - SI` as the base, so entries after 0x0F come from `_trait_option_flags[16..31]` into `param_1[15..30]`.   ```diff

  ```diff
+ for (int16_t i = 10; i < 32; ++i) {
  ```

- Function address annotation does not match the provided function.

  ```diff
+        move_info->pad = 0;
  ```

  ```diff
+                        (navigator_bonus <= 0) ? &dummy_nav_flag : nullptr,
  ```

- Final failure condition is too broad. In the binary, `out_of_range` only forces failure if earlier blackhole/navigator gating did not already short-circuit into the “blocked / special route” path; current code makes any `out_of_range` unconditionally force `turns_left = -1`.   ```diff

  Short explanation: this needs restructuring to mirror the assembly’s branch ordering around `has_stargate_route`, `uses_wormhole`, `has_jumpgate_route`, and `out_of_range`; the current flattened logic is not instruction-equivalent.

0x004362C0 SHIPMOVE::Can_Order_Ship_

fail

- Owner validation is too strict and does not match assembly. The binary only rejects `owner >= _NUM_PLAYERS` after sign-extension; it does not explicitly reject negative owners. Current code adds `ship->owner < 0`, which changes behavior for negative owners.
  ```diff
  -        if (ship->owner < 0 || ship->owner >= (int32_t)MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS) {
  +        if ((int16_t)ship->owner >= (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS) {
               return false;
           }
  ```

- Player index computation uses unsigned reinterpretation and can access the wrong player for negative owners. Assembly uses `movsx` from `char` owner and indexes `_player` with the signed value. Current `&MOX::_player[(uint8_t)ship->owner]` is not equivalent.
  ```diff
  -        s_player* player = &MOX::_player[(uint8_t)ship->owner];
  +        s_player* player = &MOX::_player[(int16_t)ship->owner];
  ```

- Relay bitmask type/shift semantics are wrong. Assembly builds the mask in an 8-bit register (`BL=1; SHL BL,CL`), so shifts are effectively 8-bit and wrap/truncate; current `uint8_t player_mask = 1 << (ship->owner & 0x1F);` performs the shift in `int`, which is not the same for counts >= 8 and hides original flags-dependent behavior.
  ```diff
  -            uint8_t player_mask = 1 << (ship->owner & 0x1F);
  +            uint8_t player_mask = 1;
  +            player_mask = (uint8_t)(player_mask << ship->owner);
  ```

- The star relay checks are swapped relative to structure offsets used by the binary. Assembly tests offset `+0x3C` first when `dist <= 5`, then offset `+0x3B` only when `dist <= 2`. With the provided packed layout, those are `has_subspace` then `has_tachyon`. Current source tests `has_subspace` for the 5-parsec case and `has_tachyon` for the 2-parsec case, which is reversed against the memory accesses.
  ```diff
  -                if (dist <= 5) {
  -                    if (star->has_subspace & player_mask) {
  +                if (dist <= 5) {
  +                    if (star->has_tachyon & player_mask) {
                           return true;
                       }
                       if (dist <= 2) {
  -                        if (star->has_tachyon & player_mask) {
  +                        if (star->has_subspace & player_mask) {
                               return true;
                           }
                       }
                   }
  ```

fail

---------------------------------------

- Owner validation is broader than the binary. The implementation rejects negative owners, but the assembly only does a signed compare against `MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS` and therefore would allow negative `ship->owner` to pass the first gate and index `_player` with a negative offset if reached. This changes control flow and memory access behavior.
  ```diff
  -        if (ship->owner < 0 || ship->owner >= (int32_t)MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS) {
  +        if ((int16_t)ship->owner >= (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_PLAYERS) {
              return false;
          }
  ```

- Relay bitmask type/shift semantics are wrong. The binary builds the mask in an 8-bit register (`MOV BL,1` / `SHL BL,CL`), so shifts are modulo 8 in practice and truncate to `byte`. The current code computes in `int` then narrows to `uint8_t`, which differs for owners outside 0..7 and is not instruction-equivalent.
  ```diff
  -            uint8_t player_mask = 1 << (ship->owner & 0x1F);
  +            uint8_t player_mask = (uint8_t)(1u << (uint8_t)ship->owner);
  ```
  Better yet, keep this concern explicit because the original operation is 8-bit-register based.

- Tech check order is not preserved. The assembly tests `TACHYON_COMMUNICATIONS` first, then `SUB_SPACE_COMMUNICATIONS`; current code checks subspace first via `||`. This matters for strict instruction-by-instruction validation and short-circuit behavior.
  ```diff
  -        if (player->tech_applications[TECH_APP_SUB_SPACE_COMMUNICATIONS] == 3 ||
  -            player->tech_applications[TECH_APP_TACHYON_COMMUNICATIONS] == 3) {
  +        if (player->tech_applications[TECH_APP_TACHYON_COMMUNICATIONS] == 3 ||
  +            player->tech_applications[TECH_APP_SUB_SPACE_COMMUNICATIONS] == 3) {
  ```

- Player indexing uses `(uint8_t)ship->owner`, but the binary uses sign-extended owner (`MOVSX`) consistently when computing the `_player` element address. Combined with the first discrepancy, this masks the original signed indexing behavior.
  ```diff
  -        s_player* player = &MOX::_player[(uint8_t)ship->owner];
  +        s_player* player = &MOX::_player[(int16_t)ship->owner];
  ```

0x00435DE0 FMTPARA::Set_Line_Limits_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The exclusion-Y overlap test is wrong. Assembly uses signed comparisons equivalent to `para_y <= y2[i] && para_y <= y1[i]`; current code preserves that unusual logic, but this is likely already suspicious semantically. More importantly, the implementation wraps it in a different outer loop structure that changes behavior.
  ```diff
  - if (py <= paragrph::_exclusion_area_y2[i] && py <= paragrph::_exclusion_area_y1[i]) {
  + if (py <= paragrph::_exclusion_area_y2[i] && py <= paragrph::_exclusion_area_y1[i]) {
  ```
  Minimal note: keep exact signed `short` comparisons, but fix surrounding control flow below.

- The exclusion processing loop does not match the binary and can spin forever. In assembly, the function:
  1) snapshots `font_height_px` into a local once,
  2) scans exclusions while `DX < _exclusion_count && BX > DI`,
  3) decrements that local each full pass,
  4) repeats only while the decremented local remains positive.
  
  Current code instead uses:
  `while (right_limit > left_limit) { ... if (font_height > 0) continue; }`
  where `font_height` is never decremented, creating a potential infinite loop and different control flow.
  ```diff
  - int16_t font_height = _style_p->font_height_px;
  - if (font_height > 0) {
  -     while (right_limit > left_limit) {
  + int16_t passes = _style_p->font_height_px;
  + while (passes > 0) {
          for (int16_t i = 0; i < (int16_t)paragrph::_exclusion_count && right_limit > left_limit; ++i) {
              ...
          }
  -       if (font_height > 0) {
  -           continue;
  -       }
  -       break;
  +       --passes;
      }
  ```

- The exclusion-edge update logic is not instruction-for-instruction equivalent and conflates temporaries. Assembly preserves three distinct working values:
  - `BX` = current right limit,
  - `DI` = current left limit,
  - `SI` = best exclusion right edge candidate,
  - local_2 = best exclusion left edge candidate.
  
  Current code rewrites these through nested `if`s with different assignment ordering, which risks different outcomes in edge cases.
  ```diff
  - if (left_limit < x2_ex) {
  -     if (right_limit > x2_ex) {
  -         right_limit = x2_ex;
  -     } else if (excl_right_x > x2_ex) {
  -         excl_right_x = x2_ex;
  -     }
  - }
  + if (left_limit < x2_ex) {
  +     if (right_limit > x2_ex) {
  +         right_limit = x2_ex;
  +     }
  + } else if (right_limit <= x2_ex && excl_right_x > x2_ex) {
  +     excl_right_x = x2_ex;
  + }
  ```
  and similarly for the `x1_ex` half:
  ```diff
  - if (x1_ex > left_limit) {
  -     if (right_limit > x1_ex) {
  -         left_limit = x1_ex;
  -     } else if (right_limit <= x1_ex && excl_right_x > x1_ex) {
  -         excl_left_x = x1_ex;
  -     }
  - } else if (right_limit <= x1_ex && excl_right_x > x1_ex) {
  -     excl_left_x = x1_ex;
  - }
  + if (x1_ex > left_limit) {
  +     if (right_limit > x1_ex) {
  +         left_limit = x1_ex;
  +     }
  + } else if (right_limit <= x1_ex && excl_right_x > x1_ex) {
  +     excl_left_x = x1_ex;
  + }
  ```

- Type/termination control around `_exclusion_count` is fragile. Assembly uses signed loop compare `CMP DX, word ptr [_exclusion_count]` with `JGE`, after an initial `JLE 0` gate. Current declaration is `uint16_t`, then casted in the loop. This can misrepresent original signed semantics if corrupt/negative values are ever observed.
  ```diff
  - inline uint16_t& _exclusion_count = *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(...);
  + inline int16_t& _exclusion_count = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(...);
  ```

Minimal note: at least validate signedness against all users before changing globally.

The current implementation is therefore not safe to validate as matching the binary.


Assembly stores `0x16` in a stack slot before loops and later reads a sequence of 16-bit values from contiguous stack memory beginning there (`22, 0, 0, 0, ...`) to drive block sizes. Current code hardcodes `limit = (stack_vars[2] == 0) ? 22 : 0;`, which is only an approximation of current observed behavior, not a faithful implementation of the actual memory-driven loop.

Current code compresses this into a simplified boolean flow that is harder to verify against the branch structure and reuses `stack_vars[3]`/`k` in a way that obscures the original per-entry indexing.

0x00433120 FMTPARA::Process_Paragraph_Text_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Signedness bug in character classification/case conversion path. The assembly zero-extends the source byte (`MOV AL, BL`) before `__isctype`, `_toupper`, and `_tolower`, but the implementation uses `char c` directly with `isalpha(c)`, `_toupper(c)`, `_tolower(c)`, and indexes `char_widths[c]`.   Suggested diff:
  ```cpp

  uint8_t uc = (uint8_t)c;
  ...
  uint32_t type = isalpha(uc);
  ...
  c = (char)_toupper(uc);
  ...
  c = (char)_tolower(uc);
  ...
  char_x_end = (int16_t)_style_p->char_widths[uc] + _line_p->x;
  ```

- The original uses the CRT classification mask `0x103` (`__isctype(..., 0x103)` or locale table lookup), not plain `isalpha`. `0x103` includes alphabetic plus extra classification bits used by this CRT path; reducing it to `isalpha` is not instruction-accurate and may differ for locale/extended characters.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp

      type = __isctype(uc, 0x103);
  } else {
      type = (*(uint16_t*)(PTR_DAT_00561580 + uc * 2)) & 0x103;
  }
  ```

- Same classification mismatch repeats in the `_insert_case == 3` reset check after character emission; original again uses the same zero-extended `0x103` test, not `isalpha(c)`.
  Suggested diff:

  ```cpp
      if (alpha_check != 0) {
          _insert_case = 0;
      }
  }
  ```

  ```diff
  -                for (int16_t t = 0; t < draw_count; ++t) {
  +                for (int16_t t = 0; t < n_points; ++t) {
  ```

- The line drawing loop uses derived locals `y1`/`y2` and `x`, while the assembly explicitly carries forward the previous `DI` by assigning `DI = offset_x + DI` each iteration. This is subtle but part of exact control-flow/state validation, especially since `offset_x` is cumulative rather than recomputed from `t`.
  ```diff
  -                int16_t x = 0xEE;
  +                int16_t x = 0xEE;
                   for (int16_t t = 0; t < draw_count; ++t) {
  ...
  -                    line::Line_(x + 1, y1 + 1, offset_x + x + 1, y2 + 1, 0xB1);
  -                    line::Line_(x, y1, offset_x + x, y2, color);
  -                    x = offset_x + x;
  +                    int16_t next_x = offset_x + x;
  +                    line::Line_(x + 1, y1 + 1, next_x + 1, y2 + 1, 0xB1);
  +                    line::Line_(x, y1, next_x, y2, color);
  +                    x = next_x;
                   }
  ```

- There is unresolved uncertainty around the exact `itoa` symbol and calling form. The disassembly calls `__itoa`; current source uses `itoa`. This may compile to the same CRT helper depending on headers/toolchain, but without confirming the project declaration/macro, the implementation cannot be marked as exact.
  ```diff
  -        itoa(max_scale, buffer, 10);
  +        __itoa(max_scale, buffer, 10);
  ```

0x00426C00 INFO::Display_Graphed_Players_Names_

fail

- `spacing` is left uninitialized when `MOX::_active_count <= 3`, but the function still executes `x_pos += spacing` on the 3rd printed entry (`i == 2`). The binary allocates a 4-byte local and only writes its low 16 bits in the `>3` / `>6` branches; reproducing that as an uninitialized local is required, but the current source comment flags it as a bug instead of restoring the exact stack-local behavior explicitly. This leaves validation uncertain because the implementation relies on undefined C++ semantics rather than a faithful low-level reconstruction.
  ```diff
   void __cdecl Display_Graphed_Players_Names_() {
       int16_t x_pos = 351;
       int16_t y_pos = 64;
-      int16_t spacing;
-      uint16_t active_count = (uint16_t)MOX::_active_count;
+      int16_t spacing;
+      int16_t active_count = MOX::_active_count;
  ```

- `MOX::_active_count` is treated as signed 16-bit in the assembly (`MOV AX`, `CMP AX`, `TEST AX,AX`, `JLE`), but the implementation converts it to `uint16_t` up front. That changes semantics for negative values, especially in the `>3` / `>6` tests and loop entry check, and does not match the instruction flow.
  ```diff
-      uint16_t active_count = (uint16_t)MOX::_active_count;
+      int16_t active_count = MOX::_active_count;
  ```

- Active player index recovery is not typed faithfully. The assembly zeroes upper bits after loading the byte and then sign-extends from `AX`, effectively producing a non-negative 0..255 index here. Using `int8_t player_idx = (int8_t)MOX::_active_player[i];` can turn values >= 128 into negative indices, which is a signedness mismatch against the actual machine code.
  ```diff
-              int8_t player_idx = (int8_t)MOX::_active_player[i];
+              uint8_t player_idx = (uint8_t)MOX::_active_player[i];
  ```

00426920 INFO::Draw_The_History_Graph_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Horizontal tick dot X coordinate is off by 1.
  - Assembly computes `AX = DI; DEC AX; PUSH EAX; PUSH EBX; PUSH color; CALL graphics::Dot_`, so the dot is drawn at `x = BX`, `y = DI - offset - 1`.
  - Current code draws `graphics::Dot_(0xed - offset - 1, y, 0xb1);`, which swaps the changing coordinate logic and places the `-1` on X instead of Y.
  - Minimal suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -    graphics::Dot_(0xed - offset - 1, y, 0xb1);
    +    graphics::Dot_(y, 0xed - offset - 1, 0xb1);
    ```

- Horizontal tick line arguments are transposed relative to the assembly.
  - `line::Line_` is consistently used as `(x1, y1, x2, y2, color)` in this function, verified by the vertical grid and axis calls.
  - Assembly pushes `0xb9, BX, 0xef, BX, EDI`, yielding `line::Line_(EDI, BX, 0xef, BX, 0xb9)`: a short horizontal line from `x = DI` to `x = 0xef` at `y = BX`.
  - Current code uses `line::Line_(0xee - offset, y, 0xef, y, 0xb9);`, again using the wrong varying coordinate as X instead of Y.
  - Minimal suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -    line::Line_(0xee - offset, y, 0xef, y, 0xb9);
    +    line::Line_(y, 0xee - offset, 0xef, 0xee - offset, 0xb9); // if following current variable naming
    ```
    or, clearer after renaming:
    ```cpp
    +    int16_t x1 = 0xee;
    +    int16_t y1 = 0xed - offset;
    +    line::Line_(x1, y1, 0xef, y1, 0xb9);
    ```

- Variable semantics for the second loop are reversed, which masks the coordinate bug.
  - In assembly, `DI` is the X position stepping from `0xee` rightward by graph labels later, while `BX` is the Y level stepping downward by 10. The current C++ names the stepping variable `y` but then passes it as X to match neither the assembly nor the graph geometry.
  - This is not cosmetic because it directly caused the incorrect `Dot_`/`Line_` coordinate order above.
  - Minimal suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -    uint16_t y = 0x186;
    +    uint16_t grid_y = 0x186;
    ...
    -    graphics::Dot_(..., y, ...);
    -    line::Line_(..., y, ..., y, ...);
    -    y -= 10;
    -    } while (y >= 0x8c);
    +    graphics::Dot_(grid_y, ... , ...);
    +    line::Line_(grid_y, ..., 0xef, ..., ...);
    +    grid_y -= 10;
    +    } while (grid_y >= 0x8c);
    ```

fail

Discrepancies:

Current code tests volume before the loop and then only calls `RUSS::Mox_Update_()` inside the loop, skipping the second `Get_Stream_Volume_()` placement and changing side-effect timing.

  ```cpp
  if (sound::Get_Stream_Volume_() > 0) {
      int16_t current_volume;
      do {
          current_volume = (int16_t)sound::Get_Stream_Volume_();
          RUSS::Mox_Update_();
      } while (current_volume > 0);
  }
  ```

- `_jim_current_stream` is typed incorrectly in the project for this function’s use. Assembly uses `movsx ecx, word ptr [...]` before `Play_Stream_`, so the value is treated as signed 16-bit when widened. The header currently exposes it as `int16_t`, but the implementation forces `static_cast<uint16_t>`, which is a code smell and mismatches the signed data flow inferred from the binary.
  Suggested diff:

  ```cpp
  -        _jim_current_stream = static_cast<uint16_t>((clock() % 3) + 1);
  +        _jim_current_stream = (int16_t)((clock() % 3) + 1);
  ```

- There is unnecessary unsigned casting in this function around a signed 16-bit global. Given the binary writes a 16-bit word and later sign-extends it, the explicit `uint16_t` cast introduces avoidable type ambiguity and suggests the restored source type usage is off.
  Suggested diff:

0x00423080 BEAMS::XY_In_Shield_Radius_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The current source uses `abs(x1 - x2)` and `abs(y1 - y2)` on promoted `int`, but the binary performs a 16-bit subtract first (`SUB CX,...`, `SUB SI,...`), then sign-extends that 16-bit result and takes the absolute value in 32-bit. This changes overflow/wrap behavior for cases where the 16-bit subtraction overflows.
- The current implementation computes the shield radius directly as an `int16_t` from `_shield_diameters[shield_idx] >> 1`; while numerically equivalent for normal values, the binary explicitly loads an 8-bit diameter into `AL`, shifts in 8-bit, clears `AH`, then sign-extends `AX`. To match exactly, the intermediate should stay byte/16-bit sized.
- Same issue pattern is already visible nearby: `XY_In_Reflection_Field_Radius_` uses explicit `static_cast<int16_t>` on the deltas, but this function does not, which is a consistency red flag for source restoration accuracy.

Minimal suggested diff snippet:
```cpp
bool __cdecl XY_In_Shield_Radius_(int16_t x1, int16_t y1, int16_t x2, int16_t y2, int16_t ship_size) {
    int16_t shield_idx = Ship_Size_To_Shield_Size_(ship_size);
    int16_t radius = (int16_t)((uint8_t)_shield_diameters[shield_idx] >> 1);

    if (abs((int16_t)(x1 - x2)) <= radius &&
        abs((int16_t)(y1 - y2)) <= radius) {
        return true;
    }

    return false;
}
```

fail

Discrepancies:

  - Minimal follow-up needed: verify `00422e40` against its own disassembly before accepting this call site as exact.

0x0041FFA0 BEAMS::Rob_Interpolate_Line_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Final `*p_y` update does not match the instruction stream. The assembly does `ADD word ptr [EDX],SI`, i.e. adds the computed delta to the existing `*p_y`, while the current code assigns `start_y + cur_y`. Those are only equivalent if `*p_y` is guaranteed unchanged for the whole call; the binary performs an in-memory add.
  ```diff
  -        *p_y = start_y + cur_y;
  +        *p_y += (int16_t)cur_y;
  ```

- Midpoint initialization uses `/ 2` in source, but the assembly uses `SAR` (`dx >> 1`, `dy >> 1`). For negative odd values this changes behavior: `SAR` rounds toward negative infinity, C++ `/ 2` rounds toward zero.
  ```diff
  -        int32_t cur_x = dx / 2;
  -        int32_t cur_y = dy / 2;
  +        int32_t cur_x = dx >> 1;
  +        int32_t cur_y = dy >> 1;
  ```

- Because of the above signed rounding issue, the binary search loop does not match for many negative-slope cases. The loop itself is structurally correct, but correctness depends on the `SAR`-initialized half-step values.
  ```diff
  -        int32_t cur_x = dx / 2;
  -        int32_t cur_y = dy / 2;
  +        int32_t cur_x = dx >> 1;
  +        int32_t cur_y = dy >> 1;
  ```

  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  #include "funcs.h"
  // ...
  return ::s_an;
  // ...
  return ::s_a;
  ```
  or
  ```cpp
  return reinterpret_cast<char*>(0x00552418);
  // ...
  return reinterpret_cast<char*>(0x00552414);
  ```

- Header/source typing around the string symbols is inconsistent with the binary’s direct address returns. `dip_scrn.h` exposes `s__00551100` as `const char*`, while `An_` returns `char*` and uses `const_cast`. This likely compiles, but it introduces avoidable uncertainty about the exact intended symbol typing for this function.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  inline char* s__00551100 = reinterpret_cast<char*>(0x00551100);
  ```
  and then:
  ```cpp
  return s__00551100;
  ```

Analysis:
- Control flow of the body itself matches the disassembly exactly: test `_settings.language`, return empty string if nonzero; otherwise inspect only the first byte of `text`; compare against `A/a/E/e/I/i/O/o/U/u`; return `s_an` for vowels and `s_a` otherwise.
- Parameter count/calling convention match (`__cdecl`, one pointer argument).
- No extra side effects are present.
- Validation is blocked by unresolved symbol provenance/typing for the returned global pointers.

0x00420110 BEAMS::Draw_Generic_Beam_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Wrong clamp direction for the shield/reflection truncation length before the second `Rob_Interpolate_Line_`.
  - Assembly does `cap_reduce = min(duration_frames - 1, reduce_len)` via `CMP ECX,EAX / JL / MOV ECX,EAX`, but current code uses `if (reduce_len < cap_reduce) cap_reduce = reduce_len;`, which computes `min` only if variable naming aligns; here the later use and surrounding flow expose the decompiler-derived logic is not traced carefully enough and should be expressed directly from asm to avoid ambiguity.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    int16_t cap_reduce = duration_frames - 1;
    if (cap_reduce > reduce_len) {
        cap_reduce = reduce_len;
    }
    ```

- `mount_scale_mod == 0` path reverses the max-selection logic for `fx_param_4` and `fx_param_3`.
  - Assembly:
    - `if (fx_param_4 <= fx_param_2) fx_param_4 = fx_param_2;`
    - `if (fx_param_3 <= fx_param_1) fx_param_3 = fx_param_1;`
  - Current code does the opposite comparisons and can shrink instead of clamp upward.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    if (fx_param_4 < fx_param_2) {
        fx_param_4 = fx_param_2;
    }
    if (fx_param_3 < fx_param_1) {
        fx_param_3 = fx_param_1;
    }
    ```

- Ship explosion size argument is wrong.
  - Assembly passes `uVar3` / `is_palette_not_90` as the final `Draw_Ship_Explosion_` argument, matching the current code, but this is only valid because that callee expects the 2-entry explosion bank index, not ship size. However the surrounding recovered signature names it `size_idx`; this is inconsistent project typing and needs confirmation from callee semantics before validation can pass.
  - Minimal clarification diff:
    ```cpp
    // rename Draw_Ship_Explosion_ last parameter to explosion_bank or similar
    ```

- Custom FX color extraction uses `reinterpret_cast<int32_t*>` on packed byte arrays.
  - The assembly pushes raw dwords from offsets `0x00,0x04,0x08,0x0C,0x10,0x14`; current code matches bytewise layout, but the explicit casts are a code smell per project rules and may hide aliasing/packing mistakes. This needs confirmation or replacement with typed packed dword fields/helper.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    // replace reinterpret_cast loads with a helper that reads packed dwords explicitly
    ```

- Parameter typing around hit/special flags remains uncertain.
  - Assembly treats `hit_result_flags` and `special_flag` as 16-bit values with zero/nonzero tests only, while current signature uses `uint16_t` for both. This is probably fine, but because surrounding calls sometimes pass signed `int16_t`, signedness should be confirmed across the interface before declaring an exact match.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    // verify all declarations/callers use consistent 16-bit flag types
    ```

The main concrete mismatch is the `mount_scale_mod == 0` branch comparison logic; that alone is enough to fail exact validation.

0x00422D20 BEAMS::Add_Damage_Indicator_For_Ship_

fail

- Parameter types do not match the actual calling convention width from the disassembly. `param_1`, `param_2`, and especially `param_4` are consumed as 16/8-bit values from the stack (`MOV CX`, `MOV DX`, `MOV AL`), but the implementation exposes them as `int32_t`. This is not just cosmetic: callers may legally pass non-normalized values, and the original function forwards the raw low byte of `param_4` to `Add_Damage_Message_To_Queue_`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - void __cdecl Add_Damage_Indicator_For_Ship_(int32_t dx, int32_t dy, int16_t ship_idx, int32_t flag, int16_t total_damage, int16_t struct_damage)
  + void __cdecl Add_Damage_Indicator_For_Ship_(int16_t dx, int16_t dy, int16_t ship_idx, uint8_t side, int16_t total_damage, int16_t struct_damage)
  ```

- The last argument passed to `Add_Damage_Message_To_Queue_` is wrong. Assembly does `PUSH EAX` where `AL` still contains the original `param_4` byte; the current code passes `(uint8_t)(flag != 0)`, collapsing all nonzero byte values to `1`. That changes behavior if the caller passes any nonzero side code other than `1`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  -        BEAMS::Add_Damage_Message_To_Queue_(x_center, y_center, total_damage, struct_damage, 0, (uint8_t)(flag != 0));
  +        BEAMS::Add_Damage_Message_To_Queue_(x_center, y_center, total_damage, struct_damage, 0, side);
  ```

- The branch condition should test the raw byte value, not an `int32_t` semantic boolean. In assembly `TEST AL,AL` uses only the low 8 bits. With current signature, values differing outside the low byte could be observed incorrectly at the C++ level.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  -        if (flag != 0) {
  +        if (side != 0) {
  ```

- The current implementation adds explicit narrowing casts on `dx` and `dy`, which is a smell here because the original parameters are already 16-bit stack values. The casts compensate for an incorrect prototype rather than reflect source intent.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  -        x_center += static_cast<int16_t>(dx);
  -        y_center += static_cast<int16_t>(dy);
  +        x_center += dx;
  +        y_center += dy;
  ```

0x0041FFA0 BEAMS::Rob_Interpolate_Line_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The binary uses a very specific signed halving / bisection algorithm with truncation semantics derived from `cdq; sub eax,edx; sar eax,1`, but the current code uses plain C++ `/ 2` on potentially negative values. That does not match instruction-for-instruction for odd negative deltas and can shift the interpolation point by 1.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  static int32_t half_toward_zero_asm(int32_t v) {
      return (v - (v >> 31)) >> 1;
  }

  // then replace:
  cur_x += (high_x - cur_x) / 2;
  cur_y += (high_y - cur_y) / 2;
  cur_x += (low_x - cur_x) / 2;
  cur_y += (low_y - cur_y) / 2;

  // with:
  cur_x += half_toward_zero_asm(high_x - cur_x);
  cur_y += half_toward_zero_asm(high_y - cur_y);
  cur_x += half_toward_zero_asm(low_x - cur_x);
  cur_y += half_toward_zero_asm(low_y - cur_y);
  ```

- The current midpoint initialization `dx / 2` and `dy / 2` also does not reproduce the assembly’s `sar` behavior for negative odd values. The binary uses arithmetic right shift, not signed division.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  int32_t cur_x = dx >> 1;
  int32_t cur_y = dy >> 1;
  ```

- The current implementation relies on `abs(...)` from C++, while the assembly computes absolute value manually in 32-bit signed arithmetic. This is usually equivalent here, but for strict restoration/validation it introduces avoidable semantic uncertainty, especially around exact signed behavior.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  static int32_t abs32_asm(int32_t v) {
      int32_t s = v >> 31;
      return (v ^ s) - s;
  }

  int32_t max_dist = abs32_asm(dx);
  if (abs32_asm(dy) > max_dist) max_dist = abs32_asm(dy);
  ...
  int32_t cur_dist = abs32_asm(cur_x);
  if (abs32_asm(cur_y) > cur_dist) cur_dist = abs32_asm(cur_y);
  ```

0041F560 SHIPS::Draw_Directional_Multi_Colored_Line_

fail

- Signature/type mismatch on the 6th argument passed to `line::Multi_Colored_Line_`: assembly passes a sign-extended `short` or clamped signed value through `push`, but the callee is declared as `uint16_t offset`. This is not proven safe for negative `phase` inputs and obscures original signed behavior.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  // line.h / line.cpp
  - void __cdecl Multi_Colored_Line_(int16_t x1, int16_t y1, int16_t x2, int16_t y2, s_colors* colors, uint16_t offset);
  + void __cdecl Multi_Colored_Line_(int16_t x1, int16_t y1, int16_t x2, int16_t y2, s_colors* colors, int16_t offset);
  ```

- The current implementation relies on C++ promotion rules in `int16_t inv_phase = 7 - phase; if (inv_phase <= 0)`, while the assembly computes in 32-bit signed arithmetic first (`movsx`, `sub esi,eax`, `test esi,esi`, clamp only when `<= 0`). For out-of-range `phase` values this can differ due to narrowing back to `int16_t`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - int16_t inv_phase = 7 - phase;
  - if (inv_phase <= 0) {
  -     inv_phase = 0;
  - }
  + int inv_phase = 7 - (int)phase;
  + if (inv_phase <= 0) {
  +     inv_phase = 0;
  + }
  ```

- Because of the above type uncertainty, the exact flags-dependent behavior for negative or large `phase` values cannot be validated with confidence. The function body otherwise appears close, but this unresolved signedness issue requires follow-up.

0041B2E0 DIP_SCRN::Get_Net_Diplomacy_Choices_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Handshake/sync branch is reversed and argument order to `Diplomacy_Synch_Mode_` is wrong relative to the assembly.
  - Assembly:
    - if `PLAYER_NUM == talker`: `Get_Listen_Mode_Message_(listen)`, `Send_Diplomacy_Handshake_(listen, talker)`, loop `Diplomacy_Synch_Mode_(talker, listen)`
    - else: `Get_Listen_Mode_Message_(talker)`, `Send_Diplomacy_Handshake_(talker, listen)`, loop `Diplomacy_Synch_Mode_(listen, talker)`
  - Current code swaps the outer condition behavior.
  - Minimal diff:
    ```cpp
    if (MOX::_PLAYER_NUM == _talker_ambassador) {
        Get_Listen_Mode_Message_(_listen_ambassador);
        Send_Diplomacy_Handshake_(_listen_ambassador, _talker_ambassador);
        do {
            Diplomacy_Synch_Mode_(_talker_ambassador, _listen_ambassador);
        } while (_synch_up_established_flag == 0);
    } else {
        Get_Listen_Mode_Message_(_talker_ambassador);
        Send_Diplomacy_Handshake_(_talker_ambassador, _listen_ambassador);
        do {
            Diplomacy_Synch_Mode_(_listen_ambassador, _talker_ambassador);
        } while (_synch_up_established_flag == 0);
    }
    ```

- Wrong signed compare on response dispatch around `0x3E8`.
  - Assembly uses `CMP EAX,0x3e8 / JG`, i.e. signed compare after `MOVSX EAX,AX`. Current code uses `if (resp <= 1000)` / `else if (resp <= 1013)`, which changes behavior for negative `response_message` values.

    ```cpp
    if (resp > 1000) {
        if ((uint32_t)(resp - 1001) <= 12) {
            switch (resp) {
            // ...
            }
        }
    } else {
        if (resp == 1000) {
            // ...
        } else if (resp == 0x82) {
            // ...
        }
    }
    ```

- Case 1004 (`0x3EC`) does not clear `_response_message` / `_old_response_message` before calling `Get_Human_Offer_Gift_Message_`, unlike assembly.

  - Minimal diff:

    ```cpp
    _old_response_message = 0;
    Get_Human_Offer_Gift_Message_(_talker_ambassador, _net_diplomacy_message.arg1, _net_diplomacy_message.arg2);
    ```

- Player-side main menu repulsive trait check uses wrong field offset.
  - Assembly checks byte at player base + `0x8B2`, which corresponds to `traits[19]` (`0x089F + 19 = 0x08B2`), not the current symbolic `traits[19]` in project layout. Current structure layout places `traits[19]` at `0x08B2` only if the struct is exact; however the code elsewhere suggests the project’s `traits` field may not match binary layout consistently, so this needs follow-up. Validation cannot safely pass with this uncertainty.
  - Minimal diff:

    ```cpp
        MOX::_player[_listen_ambassador].traits[19] == 1) {
    ```

    or, if layout is wrong, fix the struct field placement rather than adding casts/access hacks.

- Main-menu chat call argument order is reversed.
  - Assembly pushes `talker` then `listen`, so callee receives `(listen, talker)` for cdecl right-to-left order? No: the concrete pushes are `push listen; push talker; call`, therefore first formal parameter = talker, second = listen. Current code calls `Diplomacy_Main_Net_Chat_(_listen_ambassador, _talker_ambassador)`.

- Main-menu exit/send target network player is wrong.
  - Assembly case `-1/9` sends to `MOX::_player[_listen_ambassador].network_player_id`. Current code does that correctly there, but related repulsive-net path in nearby logic sends to `MOX::_player[param_1].network_player_id` even though the same message pattern should go to the other side; this creates uncertainty for this function’s integrated behavior and should be reconciled before validation passes.
  - Minimal diff:

    ```cpp
        MOX::_player[param_2].network_player_id,
        2000,
        &_net_diplomacy_message,
        8
    );
    ```

- Several calls use `reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&_net_diplomacy_message)` while assembly passes the struct address directly as a `void*`/pointer value. This is not by itself wrong in codegen, but given the project rule about suspicious casting and the packed struct restoration goal, these casts are a code smell and may be hiding incorrect prototype use.
  - Minimal diff:

- Related signedness uncertainty remains in directly used symbol chain.
  `Current_Design_Combat_Speed_` is declared/implemented as `int8_t`, while this function zero-extends/prints the returned AL through a 32-bit integer path. That mismatch is in a directly referenced symbol and prevents full-confidence validation of the exact numeric formatting path.

0x0042F4A0 DESIGN::Check_For_Auto_Repeat_

fail

- Incorrect weapon_type index in the plus/minus checks. The assembly checks `weapon_type[i]` for the visible row, but the implementation checks `weapon_type[MOX::_first_weapon_ctr + i]`. This changes behavior when the weapon list is scrolled.
- Wrong `Weapon_Space_` call arguments for the auto-repeat plus path. The assembly passes `PLAYER_NUM, weapon_type[i], 1, weapon_specials[i], weapon_ammo[i], weapon_firing_arc[i]` in that effective order, but the current code passes firing arc as the 5th arg and `ship_size` as the 6th arg.

- `_replacement_wait_ctr` update is not explicitly tied to the left-button path in the source. In assembly it increments only when `mouse::Mouse_Button_() == 1`; on any other mouse state the function zeroes `_repeat_count` and returns without touching `_replacement_wait_ctr`.

  - Assembly computes the candidate `space_used` in 16-bit (`DX += AX`, then `MOVSX EAX,DX`) before comparing against 32-bit hull space. Current code uses full 32-bit arithmetic. That can differ on overflow/sign-extension edge cases.


- The local state mapping is semantically muddled.

- Signedness/indexing is underspecified in the implementation. 0x0042ad00 COLXPORT::N_Colonies_

fail

Discrepancies:
- `_cheezy_hack_col_count_state` is typed as `uint8_t`, but the binary uses a signed byte compare against zero only; that part is harmless. The real issue is the parameter/index path and owner compare signedness are not restored precisely enough for validation.
- `player_idx` is compared in assembly as a sign-extended 16-bit value against `owner` sign-extended from `char` (`MOVSX BP,[ESI]` then `CMP BP,DI`). Current code forces `colony->owner == (char)player_idx`, truncating the 16-bit parameter before compare. That is not the same operation for out-of-range values and is not instruction-accurate.
- Cached path indexes `_cheezy_hack_col_count` with `param_1` after `MOVSX EAX,word ptr [...]`; current code uses `_cheezy_hack_col_count[player_idx]` with no explicit sign-extended behavior guard. Since the table is only 8 entries, the restored source should reflect expected signed domain more clearly; as written, it relies on unchecked C++ indexing semantics.
- `MOX::_NUM_COLONIES` is declared as `uint16_t`, but the function uses signed comparisons (`JLE`, `JL`) against zero and loop index. That project type mismatch makes the current `for (int16_t i = 0; i < MOX::_NUM_COLONIES; ++i)` not strictly equivalent at the source level and prevents precise validation.

Minimal suggested diff snippets:
```cpp
short __cdecl N_Colonies_(short player_idx) {
    if (_cheezy_hack_col_count_state != 0) {
        return _cheezy_hack_col_count[(int16_t)player_idx];
    }

    short colony_count = 0;
    for (int16_t i = 0; i < (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_COLONIES; ++i) {
        s_colony* colony = &MOX::_colony[i];
        if ((int16_t)colony->owner == player_idx && colony->outpost_flag == 0) {
            ++colony_count;
        }
    }
    return colony_count;
}
```


0x0042C350 INVASION::Change_Pop_Ownership_

fail

- `colony_war_losses` update uses the wrong source type/sign extension. Assembly does `mov cl,[colony+0xA]` then zeroes upper byte and adds a 16-bit value, so it adds `(uint8_t)colony->n_pops`. Current code uses `(int16_t)colony->n_pops`, which sign-extends negative values.
  ```diff
  -    MOX::_player[old_owner].colony_war_losses[new_owner] += (int16_t)colony->n_pops;
  +    MOX::_player[old_owner].colony_war_losses[new_owner] += (uint8_t)colony->n_pops;
  ```

- The trait check at `traits[0] / 2 == 3` is implemented with C++ signed division semantics, but assembly uses `mov al,[...]; cbw; idiv cl` and compares the quotient in `AL`. For negative trait bytes this differs from plain promoted `int8_t / 2`. This should preserve 8-bit signed division behavior explicitly or the underlying trait type/usage needs confirmation.
  ```diff
  -    if (MOX::_player[new_owner].traits[0] / 2 == 3) {
  +    if ((int8_t)(MOX::_player[new_owner].traits[0] / (int8_t)2) == 3) {
  ```
  Minimal note: preferably rewrite to mirror signed 8-bit division behavior without relying on wider promoted arithmetic.

- BC refund for buildings 20 and 31 may not match the exact assembly rounding. The binary computes signed half as:
  `eax = cost; cdq; sub eax, edx; sar eax,1`
  i.e. signed divide-by-2 with truncation toward zero. Current code uses `cost / 2`, which only matches if compiler behavior and source type remain aligned; given the explicit assembly sequence, this is not precise enough for validation without confirming `cost` is always non-negative.
  ```diff
  -            MOX::_player[new_owner].bc += TECHDATA::_buildings[20].cost / 2;
  +            int refund = TECHDATA::_buildings[20].cost;
  +            refund = (refund - (refund >> 31)) >> 1;
  +            MOX::_player[new_owner].bc += refund;
  ```
  and likewise for building `31`.

- `new_owner` is declared `uint32_t`, but the binary uses it as an 8-bit player id in byte stores/bitfield updates and as a scaled player index expected in range 0..7. This type mismatch hides required truncation semantics and can affect comparisons/bit packing in the pop loop.
  ```diff
  -void __cdecl Change_Pop_Ownership_(int16_t colony_idx, uint32_t new_owner) {
  +void __cdecl Change_Pop_Ownership_(int16_t colony_idx, int8_t new_owner) {
  ```
  If signature cannot change due to hooks, local narrowing should be made explicit at function entry.

fail

Using an unsigned type can change behavior for negative/corrupt values and affects the exact pointer comparison path.
  Suggested diff snippet:

  ```cpp
  -     *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{ 0x00597856 });
  ```

  ```cpp
  while (colony < end) { ... }
  ```
  but the assembly recomputes `MOX::_NUM_COLONIES` and the end pointer after every iteration before the `JA` backedge. That is a control-flow and memory-access difference, not just cosmetic.

0x00434fb0 FMTPARA::FN_Set_FF_Size_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The implementation keys the reset path off `arg_ret`, but the assembly uses `param_4` only. Given the prototype, `param_4` is the 4th stack argument (`arg_ret`), so that part is fine; however the current code ignores `func_char` and `arg_index`, which is acceptable here, but it also introduces an unnecessary temporary/branch structure that obscures the exact signed absolute-value sequence from the assembly.
- The absolute value check is implemented as manual negation on `int32_t`. The binary uses `CDQ / XOR / SUB`, i.e. signed 32-bit absolute value with overflow-preserving x86 behavior. In C++, `-INT32_MIN` is undefined behavior, so this is not an exact match.
- There is an unnecessary explicit cast in `value = val + (int32_t)_para_p->para_advance_y;`. Since the field is a signed `short`, normal integer promotion already gives the correct signed `int` addition; the cast is a code smell under the restoration rules.

Minimal suggested diff snippet:
```diff

    if (arg_ret != 0) {
        _para_p->para_advance_y = _para_p->line_advance_y;
    } else {
-        int32_t value;
-        if (flag) {
-            value = val + (int32_t)_para_p->para_advance_y;
-        } else {
-            value = val;
-        }
-
-        int32_t absolute_value = value;
-        if (absolute_value < 0) {
-            absolute_value = -absolute_value;
-        }
-
-        if (absolute_value < 64 /* 0x40 */) {
+        int32_t value = flag ? val + _para_p->para_advance_y : val;
+        uint32_t sign = (uint32_t)value >> 31;
+        int32_t absolute_value = (value ^ (int32_t)-sign) - (int32_t)-sign;
+        if (absolute_value < 0x40) {
             _para_p->para_advance_y = (int16_t)value;
         }
     }
     return 0;
 }
```


- The validated function itself mirrors control flow well, but because its correctness depends on the exact raw-pop memory interpretation in `COLCALC::Colony_Pop_Prod_Produced_`, and that interface is currently mis-restored, I cannot conclusively validate it to binary precision.

00446170 AIDUDES::Collect_AI_Colonies_

- `AIDUDES::_n_our_colonies` is typed incorrectly as `uint16_t` in the project, but the binary uses `MOVSX` when reading it before index/offset math and before `_qsort`. Exact semantics are signed 16-bit.
  ```diff
  - inline uint16_t& _n_our_colonies = *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657D4});
  + inline int16_t& _n_our_colonies = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657D4});
  ```

- `AIDUDES::_n_our_blockaded` and `AIDUDES::_n_our_unblockaded` are also typed incorrectly as `uint16_t`. The function itself zeroes/increments them identically either way, but the binary reads `_n_our_blockaded` via `MOVSX` for the final pointer split, so exact signedness is part of this function’s behavior.
  ```diff

  - inline uint16_t& _n_our_unblockaded = *reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C4});
  + inline int16_t& _n_our_blockaded = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C0});
  + inline int16_t& _n_our_unblockaded = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(std::uintptr_t{0x005657C4});
  ```

- The implementation uses a high-level `for (int i = 0; i < (int)MOX::_NUM_COLONIES; ++i)` loop, while the binary computes the end pointer with signed extension and compares pointers (`JA/JBE`). This is usually equivalent only if `_NUM_COLONIES` remains non-negative and within valid range. Because the project currently types `MOX::_NUM_COLONIES` as unsigned, the exact signed control-flow semantics of the original are not preserved well enough for strict validation.
  ```diff

  + s_colony* colony_ptr = MOX::_colony;
  + s_colony* end = MOX::_colony + (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_COLONIES;
  + while (colony_ptr < end) {
        ...
  - }
  +     ++colony_ptr;
  + }
  ```

004467E0 AIDUDES::Keep_Ship_Designs_Up_To_Date_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Colony index signedness is wrong. The assembly zero-extends the `colony_idx` byte (`MOV CL,[EBP]` after `XOR ECX,ECX`), but the implementation uses `int8_t colony_idx` directly as an array index. Values >= 128 would become negative, which does not match the binary.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  -    int8_t colony_idx = (*aidudes::_our_colonies)[i].colony_idx;
  -    short producing_id = MOX::_colony[colony_idx].producing[0];

  +    int16_t producing_id = MOX::_colony[colony_idx].producing[0];
  ```

- Ship size signedness is wrong in the design-slot selection path. The binary does `MOV AL,[EDI+0x10]` then `CMP AL,5` with signed branch `JGE`, and for the `< 5` path it sign-extends with `MOVSX ECX,AL`. Current code uses plain `char` comparison and direct index cast to `int`, which is implementation-defined by host `char` signedness and may diverge from Win32/x86 behavior.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp

  -                    source_design = &aidudes::_p_ai->ship_designs[(int)ship.d.size];
  +                int8_t size = ship.d.size;
  +                if (size < 5 /* SIZE_DOOMSTAR */) {
  +                    source_design = &aidudes::_p_ai->ship_designs[(int)size];
  ```

- Lazy Doom Star cache sentinel is only partially initialized. The binary explicitly zeroes only the local design's `cost` field and later tests that same field as the loaded/not-loaded flag. A full local object with only `cost = 0` leaves the rest uninitialized until first load, which is fine only because it is never read before the load; however the current code should preserve the exact sentinel intent using `int16_t` for `cost` and avoid relying on host `short`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  -        s_ship_design doom_star_design;
  -        doom_star_design.cost = 0;
  +        s_ship_design doom_star_design;
  +        doom_star_design.cost = (int16_t)0;
  ```

Main issue requiring follow-up is the incorrect signedness on `colony_idx` and potentially `size`, which affects memory accesses and exact Win32 behavior.

00446F60 PLNTSUM::Draw_Nearest_Ship_Directional_Line_

The binary pushes marker X then marker Y, and star X+3 then star Y+4; current code calls `Range_(marker_y, marker_x, star_y + 4, star_x + 3)`. Since `Range_` is not symmetric under axis swap when mixed this way with later draw coordinates, this is a real behavior mismatch.
  ```diff

  + dist = special::Range_(marker_x, marker_y, star_x + 3, star_y + 4);
  ```

- The ship-status flag is inverted semantically from the assembly. The binary does `CMP status,1 / SETZ`, stores that byte, and later skips drawing when that byte is nonzero, i.e. the stored flag means `status == 1`. Current code stores `status != 1` and then requires it to be true. This happens to produce equivalent branch behavior here, but the variable meaning is opposite and obscures the instruction-level match; this should be expressed exactly to avoid future misuse.
  ```diff
  - bool ship_status_not_one = (MOX::_ship[ship_idx].status != 1);
  + bool ship_status_is_one = (MOX::_ship[ship_idx].status == 1);

  - if (colony_active && ship_idx > -1 && ... && ship_status_not_one) {
  + if (colony_active && ship_idx > -1 && ... && !ship_status_is_one) {
  ```

- `_can_reach_star` is declared as `uint32_t[18]`, but this function performs byte indexing exactly as `byte ptr [star_idx + 0x565848]`. Current code works only by explicit `reinterpret_cast<int8_t*>`, which is a strong sign the global type is wrong in the project. This should be a byte array type, not a dword array with ad-hoc casts.

0x00448230 PLNTSUM::Get_ETA_To_Star_

- Control flow mismatch: the current implementation breaks out of the loop as soon as any matching star is found, while the binary continues scanning all rows and keeps the larger of `best_colony_ship_idx` / `best_outpost_ship_idx` from the last matching summary row.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  int16_t __cdecl Get_ETA_To_Star_(int32_t star_idx) {
      int16_t best_ship = -1;

      if (MOX::_scroll_bar->total_rows > 0) {
          for (int16_t i = 0; i < MOX::_scroll_bar->total_rows; ++i) {
              int16_t planet_idx = _planet_summary[i].explored;
              if ((int16_t)MOX::_planet[planet_idx].star_index == (int16_t)star_idx) {
                  int16_t colony_ship = _planet_summary[i].best_colony_ship_idx;
                  int16_t outpost_ship = _planet_summary[i].best_outpost_ship_idx;
                  best_ship = colony_ship;
                  if (colony_ship <= outpost_ship) {
                      best_ship = outpost_ship;
                  }
              }
          }
      }

      return (best_ship != -1) ? HAROLD::N_Turns_To_Star_(best_ship, (int16_t)star_idx) : -1;
  }
  ```

- Dataflow/selection mismatch: the disassembly initializes `DX = -1` once, and after the first match the `JNZ` path skips all further summary reads and immediately calls `HAROLD::N_Turns_To_Star_`; however when no match has occurred yet it keeps searching. The current source models this as “find first match then break”, which does not preserve the binary’s odd loop structure and can yield different behavior if multiple rows for the same star exist.

  ```diff

  +    int8_t ship_summary_idx;
  +    short scan_highlight_pulse;
  ```


- The scan/click call arguments to `Scan_Summary_Box_Fields_` are reversed relative to the binary. Assembly pushes `last_scanned_status`, then clicked/input id in `ESI`, then scanned field in `EBP`, matching `(scanned_input, clicked_input, &last)`. Current function call uses `(current_scanned, input_id, &last)` but the local variable naming around `input_id`/`scanned_id` is inverted earlier, creating ambiguity and likely mismatch with surrounding logic.

  ```diff
  +        Scan_Summary_Box_Fields_(current_scanned, clicked_id, &last_scanned_status);
  ```

- The function implementation relies on the currently broken `Check_For_Colony_And_Outpost_Ships_` logic in this file, which sets `_found_colony_ship` for outpost ships instead of `_found_outpost_ship`. Since this function immediately consumes those globals to initialize button state, validation cannot pass while the referenced side effect source is wrong.
  ```diff

  +                            _found_outpost_ship = 1;
  ```

- There is unresolved uncertainty around packed-field handling in `s_plntsum_planet` because the code writes byte/word fields at overlapping odd offsets (`0x17`, `0x19`, etc.) while the declared structure uses multiple `short` members that do not cleanly model those accesses. This must be reconciled before this function can be considered accurately validated.

  ```diff
  -    int8_t scan_highlight_pulse_step;
  ```

00449930 PLNTSUM::Determine_Button_Status_

fail

Discrepancies:
- First branch condition order is reversed. Assembly requires `Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()` first, then `Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_()`. Current code checks colony first, which changes call order and matters for strict validation.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - if (Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_() && Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()) {
  + if (Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_() && Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_()) {
  ```

- Middle branch is reconstructed incorrectly. Assembly does:
  1) call colony, if false go to final branch
  2) call outpost, if true go to final branch
  3) otherwise set `_draw_colony_button_status = 2`, `_send_choice = 0`, return
  Current code instead tests outpost first and then colony, which implements different control flow.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - if (Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()) {
  -     if (!Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_()) {
  + if (Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_()) {
  +     if (!Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()) {
            _draw_colony_button_status = 2;
            _send_choice = 0;
            return;
        }
  ```

- Final branch is wrong. Assembly performs:
  1) call colony; if true, exit
  2) call outpost; if false, exit
  3) set `_draw_outpost_button_status = 2`, `_send_choice = 1`
  Current code uses `else if (Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_())` after an outpost check, so it repeats the outpost predicate in the wrong place and completely omits the required preceding colony re-check.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - } else if (Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()) {
  + }
  +
  + if (!Send_Colony_Button_Selectable_() && Send_Outpost_Button_Selectable_()) {
        _draw_outpost_button_status = 2;
        _send_choice = 1;
    }
  ```

- Required repeated calls are missing/misplaced. The assembly intentionally calls the selectable helpers multiple times instead of reusing prior results. Current source collapses/reorders those checks, so it does not match the binary’s exact side-effect/call behavior.

0044C510 CMBTAI::Expected_Weapon_Damage_

- Discrepancy: `range == 0` handling is inverted versus the binary. In the assembly, `range == 0` skips arc/on/off/ammo/range checks and forces `range = 1`, preserving the original weapon `count`. Current code does the opposite: when `range != 0` it performs those checks and zeroes `count`, which changes damage estimation for all callers using nonzero range.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  if (range != 0) {
      // arc/on/off/ammo/range validation, possibly set count = shots_left
  } else {
      range = 1;
  }
  ```

- Discrepancy: the special-rack case switch is incomplete/wrongly merged. The binary has distinct handlers for weapon IDs `0x23`, `0x24`, `0x26`, `0x2C`, and `0x2D`; current code incorrectly groups `0x2D` with `0x23/0x26`. In assembly, `0x2D` uses `(avg - shield) * count * 4`, while `0x2C` uses `(avg - shield) * (size+1) * count * 4`, and `0x24` uses `(avg - shield) * (size+1) * count`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  case 0x23:
  case 0x26:
      expected_damage = (avg_dam - blocked) * count * 4;
      break;
  case 0x24:
      expected_damage = (avg_dam - blocked) * size_mult * count;
      break;
  case 0x2C:
      expected_damage = (avg_dam - blocked) * size_mult * count * 4;
      break;
  case 0x2D:
      expected_damage = (avg_dam - blocked) * count * 4;
      break;
  ```

- Discrepancy: current implementation precomputes `blocked` and `size_mult` unconditionally for all rack-5 weapons. The binary only reads target `shield_type`/`size_class` inside specific cases. This is observably wrong for `target_type != 0` missile targets, where the assembly path for case `0x21` does not touch `_combat_data[target_idx].shield_type` or `.size_class`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  switch (weapon_id) {
      case 0x21:
          // do not read target ship shield_type/size_class here
          ...
          break;
      case 0x23:
      case 0x24:
      case 0x26:
      case 0x2C:
      case 0x2D: {
          int blocked = TECHDATA::_shields[MOX::_combat_data[target_idx].shield_type].shield_class;
          ...
          break;
      }
      case 0x0D:
      case 0x22: {
          int size_mult = MOX::_combat_data[target_idx].size_class + 1;
          ...
          break;
      }
  }
  ```

- Discrepancy: type/signedness does not match the instruction semantics closely enough in a few key places. The binary sign-extends `count`, `shots_left`, `arc`, `type`, `min_damage`, `max_damage`, `size_class`, and `shield_type`-derived values from packed byte/word fields. Current code uses plain `int` temporaries sourced from unsigned-typed fields like `count`, which can mask sign-extension bugs and is not faithful to the assembly.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  int16_t count = (int8_t)MOX::_combat_data[source_idx].weapons[weapon_slot].count;
  int8_t rack = (int8_t)TECHDATA::_weapons[weapon_id].type;
  int16_t min_dam = (int16_t)TECHDATA::_weapons[weapon_id].min_damage;
  int16_t max_dam = (int16_t)TECHDATA::_weapons[weapon_id].max_damage;
  ```

  ```cpp
  short mod1 = 0, mod2 = 0;
  CMBTFIRE::Get_Beam_Weapon_Modifiers_(..., &mod1, &mod2);
  ```

- The source uses a cached `ship` pointer across calls that may mutate combat state, while the binary repeatedly recomputes `MOX::_combat_data[MOX::_cur_ship]` for nearly every access. This is usually fine, but in this function multiple calls can destroy/capture/retarget units mid-turn. For strict validation, this aliasing difference creates uncertainty about stale-pointer behavior after side-effect-heavy calls.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  // Prefer direct indexed accesses at each use:
  MOX::_combat_data[MOX::_cur_ship].ai_range_tactic = ...;
  ```

0x0044DE30 CMBTAI::Target_Missile_Value_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Range gating uses the wrong parameter. The assembly tests `param_3` (`CMP [esp+param_3], 0`) to decide whether to compute `Range_To_Missile_`, but the current code tests `missile_idx > 0` and ignores `param_3`. This changes behavior for `param_3 <= 0` and for missile index `0`.
  ```cpp
  // current
  if (m_idx > 0) {

  // expected
  if (param_3 > 0) {
  ```

- The multiply/halve path does not preserve 16-bit signed overflow semantics. The binary does `IMUL BP,BX`, keeps the low 16 bits, then for non-fighters sign-extends that 16-bit result and divides by 2 with signed rounding toward zero. The current code performs the product in 32-bit and halves that 32-bit value, which diverges on overflow.
  ```cpp
  // current
  int32_t final_value = (int32_t)(weapon_damage - shield_blocked) * effective_missile_hits;
  if (TECHDATA::_weapons[missile->type].type != 4) {
      final_value /= 2;
  }

  // expected shape
  int16_t tmp = (int16_t)((weapon_damage - shield_blocked) * effective_missile_hits);
  int32_t final_value = tmp;
  if (TECHDATA::_weapons[missile->type].type != 4) {
      final_value /= 2;
  }
  ```

0044e740 CMBTAI::Auto_Move_Ship_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Missing `CMBTMOV1::Move_Cost_` call before `Move_Ship_`.
  - Assembly calls `Move_Cost_(cur_ship, chosen_x, chosen_y)` and discards the return value, then separately calls `Move_Ship_`. Current code only calls `Move_Ship_`.
  - This matters because `Move_Cost_` may have side effects through called helpers or expected sequencing, and validation requires matching call flow.
  - Minimal suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    int16_t mx = move_x_coords[rnd_idx];
    int16_t my = move_y_coords[rnd_idx];
    CMBTMOV1::Move_Cost_(cur_ship_idx, mx, my);
    int16_t movement_used = CMBTMOV1::Move_Ship_(cur_ship_idx, mx, my);
    ```

- Candidate move arrays are swapped relative to assembly stack layout.
  - Assembly stores one chosen coordinate list at `[esp+0x18]` and the other at `[esp+0x40]`, then passes them to `Move_Cost_`/`Move_Ship_` in the order fetched from those exact locations. During candidate evaluation it passes `EDI` then `ESI` into `Evaluate_Possible_Move_`, where `EDI` is the inner-loop index `0..67` and `ESI` is the outer-loop index `0..80`.
  - Current code names arrays `move_y_coords` and `move_x_coords`, passes `gx` then `gy`, and later uses `mx = move_x_coords[...]`, `my = move_y_coords[...]`. This does not clearly match the actual storage/order implied by the assembly and may invert coordinates.
  - Minimal suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    int16_t move_val1[20];
    int16_t move_val2[20];
    ...
    score_limit = CMBTAI::Evaluate_Possible_Move_(
        dist_from_hex,
        gy,
        gx,
        (int16_t)MOX::_combat_data[ship_param].ai_range_tactic,
        &num_possible_moves,
        move_val1,
        move_val2,
        score_limit
    );
    ...
    int16_t target_x = move_val1[rnd_idx];
    int16_t target_y = move_val2[rnd_idx];
    CMBTMOV1::Move_Cost_(cur_ship_idx, target_x, target_y);
    int16_t movement_used = CMBTMOV1::Move_Ship_(cur_ship_idx, target_x, target_y);
    ```
  - User follow-up is needed here because the project’s `MOX::_combat_grid[gx][gy]` indexing convention must be reconciled with the assembly’s linearized indexing before this can be certified.

- `ship_param` usage is suspicious/inexact.
  - Assembly ignores `param_1` for all current-ship accesses and uses it only when loading the `ai_range_tactic` byte for the `Evaluate_Possible_Move_` call. Current function mirrors that, but because the caller passes `MOX::_cur_ship`, this is probably harmless. Still, the restoration is semantically odd enough that without confirming surrounding ABI expectations, this remains uncertain.
  - Minimal suggested diff if normalization is intended:
    ```cpp
    (int16_t)cur_ship->ai_range_tactic
    ```
  - However this should only be changed if confirmed elsewhere, since the binary does read `param_1` there.

0x0044F010 CMBTAI::Get_Effective_Missile_Strength_

fail

Current code performs an extra `missile_counts[current_ordinal]` read in a combined condition after conversion, which is unsafe once ordinal advances past the intended active range and does not preserve the original evaluation order.
