+ while (current_ordinal <= 6 && remaining_pd_count > 0) {
      ...
-     if (missile_counts[current_ordinal] == 0 || current_ordinal > 6 || remaining_pd_count <= 0) {
+     if (current_ordinal > 6 || remaining_pd_count <= 0 || missile_counts[current_ordinal] == 0) {
          break;
      }
  }
  ```

- The final damage accumulation uses a signed check on `max_damage` for the damper-field case: assembly does `MOVSX` from `_weapons[].max_damage`, then arithmetic round-toward-zero divide by 4. Current code tests `TECHDATA::_weapons[msl_id].max_damage < 0`, but the field is declared `uint16_t`, so this condition is always false and does not reflect the signed 16-bit load in the binary.

In this function that affects exact control flow for rack filtering and missile/fighter handling. This needs confirmation from adjacent restored code/types before validation can pass.
  ```diff

0044f640 CMBTAI::Average_Missile_Kills_

- Return type/signature mismatch: assembly returns `AX`/`short`; project declares and defines `int32_t`. This is not cosmetic because caller expectations and promotion behavior differ from the original ABI.
  ```diff
  - int32_t __cdecl Average_Missile_Kills_(int16_t ship_idx, int16_t weapon_slot_idx, int16_t msl_count, int16_t msl_type, int16_t* remaining_pd_count, int16_t param6);
  + int16_t __cdecl Average_Missile_Kills_(int16_t ship_idx, int16_t weapon_slot_idx, int16_t msl_count, int16_t msl_type, int16_t* remaining_pd_count, int16_t param6);
  ```

- Comparison against `param6` is wrong in source. Assembly does `CMP AX,0xFFFF / JLE`, i.e. signed `param6 <= -1` selects fixed range `2`; otherwise it calls `Range_To_Missile_`. Current code uses `if (param6 > -1)` which is logically equivalent, but the decompiler input says `if (param_6 < 0)` and the real assembly behavior is specifically `<= -1`. This is acceptable logically; no issue by itself. The actual discrepancy is elsewhere below.

- Point-defense remaining count update is reversed at the end. Assembly:
  - computes `kills = current_health / divisor`
  - if `param_3 < kills`, stores `current_health - param_3 * divisor`, returns `param_3`
  - else stores `0`, returns `kills`
  
  Current code does:
  ```cpp
  kills = current_health / divisor;
  if (msl_count < kills) {
      *remaining_pd_count = current_health - (msl_count * divisor);
      return msl_count;
  } else {
      *remaining_pd_count = 0;
  }
  ```
  This condition is correct, but the function returns `int32_t`, not `int16_t`, and more importantly the local/temporary typing around `current_health`, `divisor`, and `kills` is not constrained to the original 16-bit behavior. The binary performs 16-bit signed division and stores in 16-bit registers throughout (`DI`, `BX`, `SI`, `AX`), so overflow/truncation semantics matter.

  Minimal correction:
  ```diff
  - int32_t __cdecl Average_Missile_Kills_(...)
  + int16_t __cdecl Average_Missile_Kills_(...)
  ```

- `base_dmg` is typed as `int32_t`, but assembly keeps this value in `BP` as a 16-bit quantity after `IMUL BP,BP,0x5` and subsequent 16-bit add. Using wider arithmetic can hide original signed wrap/truncation behavior.
  ```diff
  - int32_t base_dmg = (TECHDATA::_weapons[slot_weapon_id].min_damage + TECHDATA::_weapons[slot_weapon_id].max_damage) * 5;
  + int16_t base_dmg = (int16_t)((TECHDATA::_weapons[slot_weapon_id].min_damage + TECHDATA::_weapons[slot_weapon_id].max_damage) * 5);
  ```

- `current_health` is read from `*remaining_pd_count` as `int16_t`, but subsequent arithmetic uses mixed-width C expressions instead of preserving the binary’s 16-bit signed semantics around `IDIV BX`. This needs explicit 16-bit locals/return type to match original behavior precisely.
  ```diff
  - int16_t current_health = *remaining_pd_count;
  - int16_t divisor;
  - kills = current_health / divisor;
  + int16_t current_health = *remaining_pd_count;
  + int16_t divisor;
  + int16_t kills = current_health / divisor;
  ```

fail

- Signature mismatch: binary takes 2 parameters, current code takes 3 and relies on caller-supplied `incident`. The assembly reloads `diplomacy_incident[player][other]` from `MOX::_player` internally for all control flow. Current implementation can diverge if caller passes a stale or wrong `incident`.
  ```diff
- void __cdecl Determine_Good_Message_(int16_t player_idx, int16_t other_player_idx, int incident)
+ void __cdecl Determine_Good_Message_(int16_t player_idx, int16_t other_player_idx)
  {
+     uint8_t incident = (uint8_t)MOX::_player[player_idx].diplomacy_incident[other_player_idx];
      ...
  ```

- Probability setup differs: binary uses two independent checks
  `if (incident == 0x0F) chance = 10;`
  `if (incident == 0x10) chance = 20;`
  not `else if`. While mutually exclusive for current incident values, the current source does not mirror control flow precisely.
  ```diff
- if (incident == 15) {
-     chance = 10;
- } else if (incident == 16) {
-     chance = 20;
- }
+ if (incident == 15) {
+     chance = 10;
+ }
+ if (incident == 16) {
+     chance = 20;
+ }
  ```

- Message-incident source of truth mismatch: binary switch dispatches on the stored byte at offset `0x64F` after clearing threats, not on a local parameter. Current function’s switch is driven by the passed `incident`, so memory access behavior does not match.
  ```diff
- switch (incident) {
+ switch ((uint8_t)MOX::_player[player_idx].diplomacy_incident[other_player_idx]) {
  ```

- Type/width mismatch on incident: binary reads a byte and uses unsigned range logic (`SUB 0xC; CMP 4; JA default`). Current code uses signed `int incident`, which is broader than the actual stored field and can mask caller errors.
  ```diff
- void __cdecl Determine_Good_Message_(int16_t player_idx, int16_t other_player_idx, int incident)
+ void __cdecl Determine_Good_Message_(int16_t player_idx, int16_t other_player_idx)
  {
-     ...
+     uint8_t incident = (uint8_t)MOX::_player[player_idx].diplomacy_incident[other_player_idx];
  ```

- Caller mismatch follows from the bad signature: call sites currently pass `incident`, but binary call sequence only passes the two player indices and expects callee to fetch incident itself.
  ```diff
- Determine_Good_Message_(i, j, incident);
+ Determine_Good_Message_(i, j);
  ```

00459410 COLDRAW::Load_Road_List_Anims_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Border-condition logic is wrong for directions 2 and 3. The binary marks `anims[c] = -1` when:
  - `i == 6 && c != 0`
  - `j == 6 && c == 0`
  - `j == 6 && c > 1`
  - `i == 6 && c == 1`
  The current code simplifies this to:
  - `(i == 6 && c != 0) || (j == 6 && c != 1)`
  This incorrectly invalidates `(j == 6, c == 1)` and incorrectly leaves valid `(i == 6, c == 0)` behavior only by coincidence, while missing the exact split for `c <= 1` vs `c > 1`.

Minimal suggested diff snippet:
```cpp
for (uint8_t c = 0; c < 4; c++) {
    for (int16_t i = 0; i < 7; i++) {
        for (int16_t j = 0; j < 7; j++) {
            if (((i == 6) && (c != 0) && ((c <= 1) || (j != 6))) ||
                ((j == 6) && ((c == 0) || (c > 1))) ||
                ((i == 6) && (c == 1))) {
                _road_list[i][j].anims[c] = -1;
                _road_list[i][j].connections[c] = 0;
            } else {
                _road_list[i][j].anims[c] = anim_id++;
                _road_list[i][j].connections[c] = 0;
            }
        }
    }
}
```

More directly matching the assembly structure:
```cpp
if (((i == 6) && (c != 0)) ||
    ((j == 6) && (c == 0)) ||
    ((c > 1) && ((j == 6) || (i == 6)))) {
    _road_list[i][j].anims[c] = -1;
    _road_list[i][j].connections[c] = 0;
} else {
    _road_list[i][j].anims[c] = anim_id++;
    _road_list[i][j].connections[c] = 0;
}
```

The current source instead rejects `colony_index == -1 || outpost_flag == 0`, which changes accepted selections.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  if (_selection_state_flag == 1) {
      int16_t col_idx = MOX::_planet[planet_idx].colony_index;
      // current condition does not match 0045c24f..0045c2a0
      if (/* replace with assembly-accurate condition chain */) {
          ...
      }
  }
  ```

- Duplicate UI refresh on failure paths for states 0/1/2.  
  In the assembly, the help branches at 0045c2a2 and 0045c337 already perform:
  `COLONY::_full_draw = 1; Eric_Pop_Fields_(); Update_Selection_Fields_(); Copy_Back_To_Off_(); Draw_Selection_Popup_(); Assign_Auto_Function_();`
  and then jump to a second common redraw block at 0045c3da, causing two pop/update/draw cycles. The current source performs one inline refresh and then falls through `goto reset_ui_and_fail`, causing only one cycle.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  GENDRAW::Help_(help_text);
  COLONY::_full_draw = 1;
  Eric_Pop_Fields_();
  Update_Selection_Fields_();
  video::Copy_Back_To_Off_();
  Draw_Selection_Popup_();
  fields::Assign_Auto_Function_(Draw_Selection_Popup_, 1);
  goto common_redraw; // binary does an additional redraw sequence
  ```

- Missing `COLONY::_full_draw = 1` in state 3 gas-giant rejection path.  
  At 0045c3ad the binary does not set `_full_draw`, but then jumps to the common redraw block. However the earlier state-0/1/2 help paths explicitly set it before redraw. The current source folds all failures into one block and therefore loses the binary’s distinct side-effect pattern across branches.
  Suggested diff snippet:

  if (_selection_state_flag == 3) {
      if (MOX::_planet[planet_idx].planet_type == 3) {
          GENDRAW::Help_(ESTRINGS::E_Strings_(0x235));
          // preserve separate branch behavior instead of merging all failures
          goto reset_ui_and_fail;
      }
  }
  ```

- Control-flow structure does not preserve the assembly’s branch partitioning around `_selection_state_flag`.  
  The binary reevaluates `_selection_state_flag` multiple times and has separate exits at 0045c3c4 and 0045c407. The source collapses that into structured `if/else`, which hides an important oddity: for `_selection_state_flag == 0`, `New_Colony_Confirmation_Box_` returning nonzero exits immediately; otherwise it falls into the common redraw, while nonzero states bypass that exact branch shape. Given the incorrect state-1 logic already present, this restructuring is not trustworthy enough for validation.
  Suggested diff snippet:

  if (_selection_state_flag == 3) { ... }
  else if (_selection_state_flag == 0) {
      if (New_Colony_Confirmation_Box_(planet_idx) != 0) {
          *selection_confirmed = 1;
          return planet_idx;
      }

      *selection_confirmed = 1;
      return planet_idx;

0045CDE0 MAINPUPS::Load_Slider_Fields_

fail

Discrepancies:
- The call to `Add_Slider_Fields_` is represented with a mismatched prototype and an invented constant zero argument. The assembly pushes exactly three arguments after loading `_g_max` and `_g_min`: `&_g_var`, `_g_max`, `_g_min`, so the effective call is `Add_Slider_Fields_(_g_min, _g_max, &_g_var)`. The current source instead calls a 4-parameter function:
```cpp
void __cdecl Add_Slider_Fields_(int16_t val, int16_t padding, int16_t* ptr, int16_t limit);
...
Add_Slider_Fields_(_g_min, 0, &_g_var, _g_max);
```
Minimal suggested diff snippet:
```cpp
- void __cdecl Add_Slider_Fields_(int16_t val, int16_t padding, int16_t* ptr, int16_t limit);
+ void __cdecl Add_Slider_Fields_(int16_t min_val, int16_t max_val, int16_t* value_ptr);

- Add_Slider_Fields_(_g_min, 0, &_g_var, _g_max);
+ Add_Slider_Fields_(_g_min, _g_max, &_g_var);
```

- The callee implementation is correspondingly suspicious: it uses the second parameter only as ignored `padding` and the fourth as the upper bound. That does not match the validated caller stack layout from `0045CDE0`, so the ABI/prototype relationship around this function pair is currently inconsistent and needs confirmation/correction.
Minimal suggested diff snippet:
```cpp
- void __cdecl Add_Slider_Fields_(int16_t val, int16_t padding, int16_t* ptr, int16_t limit)
+ void __cdecl Add_Slider_Fields_(int16_t min_val, int16_t max_val, int16_t* ptr)
{
-   _mp_fields[11] = fields::Add_Scroll_Field_(..., val, limit + 1, val, limit, ..., ptr, ...);
+   _mp_fields[11] = fields::Add_Scroll_Field_(..., min_val, max_val + 1, min_val, max_val, ..., ptr, ...);
}
```

- The current declaration obscures the real stack usage and makes validation of `Load_Slider_Fields_` impossible to accept with precision, because a `__cdecl` caller/callee pair must agree on argument count and order even if some values are ignored in C++.

      ...
-     int8_t num_items;
+     uint8_t num_items;
      ...
  };
  ```

The assembly compares `app_count` as an unsigned byte (`MOV BL,[ECX+0x49]`, `CMP EBX,EDI`, `JA`), and then uses the final index to read `app_y_table[app_count]`. Current code uses `uint8_t i`, which is fine locally, but the underlying struct typing and nearby code should be audited consistently before validation can be accepted, because the project currently mixes signed and unsigned treatment of list counts.
  Suggested diff snippet:

      uint8_t app_count;
      ...
  };

Current code uses `int8_t stop_flag`; behavior is likely fine, but because other functions test it as nonzero and page-boundary logic is very flag-sensitive, this should be confirmed consistently across the project before declaring a precise match.

0045FF40 LIST::Add_Fields_To_List_Page_

- The field-id store is expressed via a layout-violating reinterpret cast instead of the actual member at offset 0x6E. The assembly writes to `[ESI + EBP + 0x6e]`, which is `app_ids[i]`. Current code reaches that byte range through `reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&item->app_y_offsets[16])[i]`. This is brittle and depends on packed layout rather than the real field identity.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&item->app_y_offsets[16])[i] = (uint8_t)field_id;
  + item->app_ids[i] = (uint8_t)field_id;
  ```

- The item-count/end computation uses signed `num_items` directly: `&state->items[state->num_items]`. Assembly zero-extends the byte at offset `0x15` (`MOV CL, [EBX+0x15]` after `XOR ECX,ECX`), so iteration bound is based on the raw unsigned 8-bit value, not signed `int8_t`. Current code is wrong for values >= 0x80 and does not match the machine code.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - s_list_item* end_item = &state->items[state->num_items];
  + s_list_item* end_item = &state->items[(uint8_t)state->num_items];
  ```

- The loop index type is `int`, while the assembly uses a 16-bit unsigned-style induction variable in DI/BP with masking (`AND EBP,0xffff`, `AND ECX,0xffff`). Given the indexed accesses use 16-bit table lookups and byte stores, the source should use a 16-bit/unsigned-compatible index to match intent and avoid needless widening.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - for (int i = 0; i < (int)item->app_count; ++i) {
  + for (uint16_t i = 0; i < item->app_count; ++i) {
  ```

More importantly, the source does not model the assembly’s explicit `local_e/local prev` update pattern, so correctness is uncertain if `Delete_Ship_Node_` is ever changed.

Current code mixes `const int16_t player_num = MOX::_PLAYER_NUM;` with repeated `(int8_t)` casts on `char` fields. This is likely safe on typical compilers, but because `char` signedness is implementation-defined, restoration is not precise enough for validation.

                                     break;
                                 }

  - The same issue applies to the second pass and the final strategy-2 escort extraction loop.

  - Assembly repeatedly uses signed compares on sign-extended 16-bit return values from `Ship_Rough_Strength_At_Star_` (`MOVSX ... AX`) and on accumulated 32-bit sums. Current code forces many comparisons through `uint32_t`.
  - If any rough strength can exceed `32767` through type mismatch or if decompilation signedness is off elsewhere, these unsigned casts alter branch behavior.
  - Minimal safer direction is to keep these values as signed `int32_t` in this function, matching assembly behavior.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -                                        if ((uint32_t)enemy_strength2[t2] < (uint32_t)strength) {
    +                                        if (enemy_strength2[t2] < strength) {
    ...
    -                                            if ((uint32_t)total_enemy_strength[turns] > (uint32_t)strength &&
    -                                                (uint32_t)total_escort_strength > (uint32_t)strength) {
    +                                            if (total_enemy_strength[turns] > strength &&
    +                                                total_escort_strength > strength) {
    ...
    -                                                    if ((uint32_t)enemy_strength[t] > (uint32_t)strength) {
    +                                                    if (enemy_strength[t] > strength) {
    ```
  - Similar unsigned comparisons appear in the in-transit branches.

- Parameter naming/types obscure the original control-flow meaning and increase uncertainty around call-site consistency.
  - Assembly treats the 4th parameter as a signed turn limit (`CMP param_4, turns`, `JL/JGE`), but the implementation’s logic mixes `int16_t max_turns` with `int32_t` temporaries and unsigned comparisons.
  - This is a validation concern because this function is turn-limit sensitive and branches differently for `< 10`, `<= max_turns`, and `param_4 < local_4a0`.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -    int8_t __cdecl Get_Best_Escorted_Colony_Ship_(int16_t* colony_ships, int16_t* num_colony_ships, int16_t star_idx, int16_t max_turns)
    +    int8_t __cdecl Get_Best_Escorted_Colony_Ship_(int16_t* colony_ships, int16_t* num_colony_ships, int16_t star_idx, int16_t max_turns)
      {
    -        int32_t best_max_turn = 0x7FFF;
    +        int32_t best_max_turn = 0x7FFF; // signed turn threshold, matches assembly semantics
    ```
  - I am not asking to change the signature, but the current implementation does not demonstrate enough signedness discipline to certify equivalence.

- Final strategy-2 escort activation trusts `best_num_escorts` without re-validating contiguous entries exactly as the original does.
  - The assembly reconstructs the escort list from the indexed `_i_all_combat_ships` segment and writes mission `4` ship-by-ship, then updates the head index based on `local_49c` vs `local_47c`.
  - The current code simplifies this with a `for (i < best_num_escorts)` loop over `_all_ships[all_ship_idx++]` and no owner/location verification during application.
  - If upstream list state is even slightly inconsistent, behavior diverges from the original’s stricter assumptions.
  - Suggested diff:
    ```cpp
    -                if (best_num_escorts > 0) {
    -                    for (int32_t i = 0; i < best_num_escorts; ++i) {
    -                        int16_t s_idx = (*AIDATA::_all_ships)[all_ship_idx++];
    +                if (best_num_escorts > 0) {
    +                    for (int32_t i = 0; i < best_num_escorts; ++i) {
    +                        int16_t s_idx = (*AIDATA::_all_ships)[all_ship_idx++];

    +                            MOX::_ship[s_idx].location != location) {

00462760 AIMOVE::Get_Enemy_Strength_On_Turn_

- The implementation indexes the strength tables by loop index `i`, but the assembly derives the ship index from pointer arithmetic `((EBP - MOX::_ship) / 0x81)`. These are equivalent only if `sizeof(s_ship_data) == 0x81` in the compiled project; the source relies on that implicitly instead of matching the binary’s exact indexing mechanism. This is a validation gap because the project code shown does not prove packed-size correctness locally.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  int ship_idx = ((int)ship - (int)MOX::_ship) / 0x81;
  turn_strengths[0] += (*strength_table)[ship_idx][_i_ai];
  ```

- Signedness/typing is not matched precisely for the star parameter path. The assembly begins with `MOVSX` on `param_1`, and later compares/mixes signed 16-bit values (`location`, `param_1`, difference against 500/1000). The current code uses `int16_t star_id`, but then promotes it to `int32_t` in a way that obscures the exact signed-16 semantics expected from the binary trace. This is minor in practice for valid star IDs, but not exact enough for strict validation.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  int star_idx = (int)(int16_t)star_id;
  int location_diff = (int)(int16_t)ship->location - star_idx;
  ```

- The current source uses a high-level `for (int i = 0; i < num_ships; ++i)` traversal, while the assembly recomputes the end pointer from `MOX::_NUM_SHIPS` each iteration and advances by `0x81` bytes. Since this restoration task requires instruction-accurate validation, I cannot confirm exact equivalence without seeing that `s_ship_data` is definitely compiled packed to 0x81 in this build.
  Suggested diff snippet:
  ```cpp
  for (s_ship_data* ship = MOX::_ship; ship < MOX::_ship + (int16_t)MOX::_NUM_SHIPS; ++ship) {
      int ship_idx = ((int)ship - (int)MOX::_ship) / 0x81;
      ...
  }
  ```

00462D10 AIMOVE::Find_Best_Attack_From_Star_

fail

Discrepancies:
- Wrong target_info field copy-back offsets/sizes for mission 1.
  - Assembly copies 16-bit values from the local target buffer back to `param_2 + 4`, `+6`, `+8` when the best score improves and local `[+4] != -1`.
  - Current code copies bytes `target_info[2..4]`, which is the wrong region and wrong width.
  - Minimal diff:
    ```cpp
    if (puVar8 != nullptr && *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(puVar8 + 4) != -1) {
        *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(target_info + 4) = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(puVar8 + 4);
        *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(target_info + 6) = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(puVar8 + 6);
        *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(target_info + 8) = *reinterpret_cast<int16_t*>(puVar8 + 8);
    }
    ```

- The comparison guarding that copy-back is wrong.
  - Assembly checks the copied local buffer field at offset `+4` as a signed 16-bit value against `0xFFFF`.
  - Current code checks `puVar8[2] != -1`, which reads the wrong byte and changes behavior.

- Potentially unsafe/star-index signedness mismatch on indexed locals.
  - Assembly sign-extends `planet.star_index` and uses it as a signed index into the 72-byte visited array and range table.
  - Current code stores it in `int8_t`, which is closer, but then uses it directly as an array subscript without explicit sign extension to `int`. If `char`/promotion behavior differs by platform/settings, this is fragile for exact restoration.
  - Minimal diff:
    ```cpp
    int target_star = (int)MOX::_planet[planet_idx].star_index;
    ```

00463090 AIMOVE::Compute_Attack_Worthiness_

Fail:
- `is_hostile` is typed and used as `bool`, but the callee returns an `int8_t` that is added numerically to strengths. The assembly does `MOV byte ... AL`, then zero-extends that byte and adds it as an integer. Collapsing it to `bool` changes behavior if the function returns `2`.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - bool is_hostile = Player_Is_Hostile_To_Player_(_i_ai, owner);
  + uint32_t hostility_bonus = (uint8_t)Player_Is_Hostile_To_Player_(_i_ai, owner);
  ...
  - uint32_t attack_space = total_space_strength + is_hostile;
  - uint32_t attack_ground = total_bomb_strength + is_hostile;
  + uint32_t attack_space = total_space_strength + hostility_bonus;
  + uint32_t attack_ground = total_bomb_strength + hostility_bonus;
  ```

- `Player_Is_Hostile_To_Player_` in this project appears to return `2` as a real value on some paths, which is consistent with the caller’s byte-add behavior. Because this function treats the result as `bool`, validation cannot pass until the interface/use is made numerically consistent.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - bool is_hostile = Player_Is_Hostile_To_Player_(_i_ai, owner);
  + uint32_t hostility_bonus = (uint8_t)Player_Is_Hostile_To_Player_(_i_ai, owner);
  ```

- Population count handling is signed in the current implementation (`colony->n_pops < min_pop`, loop bound `i < colony->n_pops`), while the assembly zero-extends `n_pops` for comparisons and loop control in this function. That should be treated as unsigned byte semantics here.
  Suggested diff:
  ```cpp
  - if (colony->n_pops < min_pop && colony->outpost_flag == 0 && _p_ai->personality != 1 && colony->n_pops != 0) {
  -     for (int i = 0; i < colony->n_pops; ++i) {
  + uint32_t n_pops = (uint8_t)colony->n_pops;
  + if ((int32_t)n_pops < min_pop && colony->outpost_flag == 0 && _p_ai->personality != 1 && n_pops != 0) {
  +     for (uint32_t i = 0; i < n_pops; ++i) {
  ```
